What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I was working on a memorial piece for my aunt recently, and I kept getting these awful, unwanted thoughts. I kept thinking I was going to dishonor her somehow. Like, if I placed a photo in the wrong spot or chose the wrong color, it would mean I didn't love her enough. And I just... I couldn't brush them off like normal creative doubts. They just kept coming back.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel the need to do anything to make these unwanted thoughts go away or to prevent them from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I'll end up rereading the label text, or just... nudging the same objects on my work table. And I check the layout photos over and over again. I basically have to keep doing it until things finally feel settled. It's frustrating because part of me knows it's already fine, and I'm really just losing studio time doing all this checking, but I still feel like I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you have these unwanted thoughts or when you're not able to perform your checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. When those thoughts happen, I get this... this really tense feeling. And if I try to just ignore it and walk away without checking the layout or the text, it's hard to focus on anything else. I just feel kind of on edge and uneasy until I finally go back and do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing these checking behaviors in other areas of your life, outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's pretty much just when I'm working on my art, especially pieces that feel really important or personal. I don't really have those kinds of thoughts or feel the need to check things over and over when I'm just going about my day-to-day life, like at home or out running errands. It seems to be tied specifically to my studio work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are affecting your overall quality of life or relationships outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it just affects my work. I mean, I'm still getting the majority of my art done, but... the extra time I lose in the studio because of all the checking is starting to have an impact. I actually missed a grant deadline recently because I was so caught up in making sure everything was perfect. And I ended up backing out of a gallery talk I really wanted to do because I just felt too behind and stressed about the work. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of opportunities, even if it's not ruining my personal relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something that come from your own beliefs and values, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. It's pretty much just when I'm working on my art, especially pieces that feel really important or personal. I don't really have those kinds of thoughts or feel the need to check things over and over when I'm just going about my day-to-day life, like at home or out running errands. It seems to be tied specifically to my studio work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are affecting your overall quality of life or relationships outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it just affects my work. I mean, I'm still getting the majority of my art done, but... the extra time I lose in the studio because of all the checking is starting to have an impact. I actually missed a grant deadline recently because I was so caught up in making sure everything was perfect. And I ended up backing out of a gallery talk I really wanted to do because I just felt too behind and stressed about the work. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of opportunities, even if it's not ruining my personal relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something that come from your own beliefs and values, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean, they're in my head, so I know they are my own thoughts. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of my core beliefs or if they feel like they come from the outside. \n\nIt's just... this sudden worry that pops into my mind while I'm working. I wouldn't say it feels like someone else is putting the thoughts there, if that's what you mean. But beyond that, I guess I just haven't really noticed or thought about where exactly it feels like it's coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about possibly dishonoring your aunt feel like they align with your own beliefs and values, or do they feel alien or contradictory to who you are?\n\nPatient: I guess... I mean, I know they're my thoughts, like I said. But I wouldn't really say they line up with who I am or what I actually believe. It's more like... they just show up, and they feel really out of place. Like, I know I'm honoring her by making the art in the first place, so the thought that I'm somehow ruining it or disrespecting her doesn't make any logical sense to me. It's just a worry that gets stuck there, even though it goes against what I actually know is true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the need to perform any of these checking behaviors outside of your studio or when you're not working on your art?\n\nPatient: No, um... not really. It's pretty much just confined to the studio. Like, when I'm at home or just out doing regular things, I don't get that urge to go back and check things over and over. It really only happens when I'm working on those specific pieces. Outside of my art, I haven't noticed anything like that at all.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the level of distress these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors cause you, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess I'm not entirely sure how to put a number on it. It's definitely stressful when it's happening in the studio, but... maybe a 3 or a 4? It's frustrating and it slows me down, but it's not like it's completely ruining my life or keeping me from functioning, you know? It's just a really annoying thing I have to deal with when I'm working.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're in conflict with your sense of self?\n\nPatient: I think... well, like I mentioned earlier, they definitely feel like they're in conflict with who I am. I don't want to be doing this, and I don't actually believe I'm dishonoring her. So, yeah, it doesn't feel like it's a part of my actual personality or my values at all. It just feels like this annoying habit or glitch that gets in the way when I'm trying to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unwanted, recurring thoughts outside of the thoughts about dishonoring your aunt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the stuff about my aunt. I haven't noticed any other thoughts like that popping up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something you'd expect to think, or do they feel like they're coming from a part of you that you don't identify with?\n\nPatient: Well, I think that's kind of what I meant just a minute ago. I definitely don't identify with them. It's not something I would ever expect myself to think, because I know I'm not actually trying to disrespect her. So... yeah, it just feels like it's coming from somewhere else. It really doesn't feel like me at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I was working on a memorial piece for my aunt recently, and I kept getting these awful, unwanted thoughts. I kept thinking I was going to dishonor her somehow. Like, if I placed a photo in the wrong spot or chose the wrong color, it would mean I didn't love her enough. And I just... I couldn't brush them off like normal creative doubts. They just kept coming back.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel the need to do anything to make these unwanted thoughts go away or to prevent them from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I'll end up rereading the label text, or just... nudging the same objects on my work table. And I check the layout photos over and over again. I basically have to keep doing it until things finally feel settled. It's frustrating because part of me knows it's already fine, and I'm really just losing studio time doing all this checking, but I still feel like I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you have these unwanted thoughts or when you're not able to perform your checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. When those thoughts happen, I get this... this really tense feeling. And if I try to just ignore it and walk away without checking the layout or the text, it's hard to focus on anything else. I just feel kind of on edge and uneasy until I finally go back and do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing these checking behaviors in other areas of your life, outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's pretty much just when I'm working on my art, especially pieces that feel really important or personal. I don't really have those kinds of thoughts or feel the need to check things over and over when I'm just going about my day-to-day life, like at home or out running errands. It seems to be tied specifically to my studio work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are affecting your overall quality of life or relationships outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it just affects my work. I mean, I'm still getting the majority of my art done, but... the extra time I lose in the studio because of all the checking is starting to have an impact. I actually missed a grant deadline recently because I was so caught up in making sure everything was perfect. And I ended up backing out of a gallery talk I really wanted to do because I just felt too behind and stressed about the work. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of opportunities, even if it's not ruining my personal relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something that come from your own beliefs and values, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean, they're in my head, so I know they are my own thoughts. I wouldn't say it feels like someone else is putting the thoughts there, if that's what you mean. But beyond that, I guess I just haven't really noticed or thought about where exactly it feels like it's coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about possibly dishonoring your aunt feel like they align with your own beliefs and values, or do they feel alien or contradictory to who you are?\n\nPatient: I guess... I mean, I know they're my thoughts, like I said. But I wouldn't really say they line up with who I am or what I actually believe. It's more like... they just show up, and they feel really out of place. Like, I know I'm honoring her by making the art in the first place, so the thought that I'm somehow ruining it or disrespecting her doesn't make any logical sense to me. It's just a worry that gets stuck there, even though it goes against what I actually know is true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the need to perform any of these checking behaviors outside of your studio or when you're not working on your art?\n\nPatient: No, um... not really. It's pretty much just confined to the studio. Like, when I'm at home or just out doing regular things, I don't get that urge to go back and check things over and over. It really only happens when I'm working on those specific pieces. Outside of my art, I haven't noticed anything like that at all.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the level of distress these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors cause you, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess I'm not entirely sure how to put a number on it. It's definitely stressful when it's happening in the studio, but... maybe a 3 or a 4? It's frustrating and it slows me down, but it's not like it's completely ruining my life or keeping me from functioning, you know? It's just a really annoying thing I have to deal with when I'm working.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're in conflict with your sense of self?\n\nPatient: I think... well, like I mentioned earlier, they definitely feel like they're in conflict with who I am. I don't want to be doing this, and I don't actually believe I'm dishonoring her. So, yeah, it doesn't feel like it's a part of my actual personality or my values at all. It just feels like this annoying habit or glitch that gets in the way when I'm trying to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unwanted, recurring thoughts outside of the thoughts about dishonoring your aunt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the stuff about my aunt. I haven't noticed any other thoughts like that popping up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something you'd expect to think, or do they feel like they're coming from a part of you that you don't identify with?\n\nPatient: Well, I think that's kind of what I meant just a minute ago. I definitely don't identify with them. It's not something I would ever expect myself to think, because I know I'm not actually trying to disrespect her. So... yeah, it just feels like it's coming from somewhere else. It really doesn't feel like me at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the urge to perform these checking behaviors when you're not thinking about your aunt or working on a memorial piece?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It really only happens when I'm working on those pieces. Like, I’ll be in the studio and I’ll end up rereading the label text, or... nudging the exact same objects on my work table. I'll check the layout photos over and over again until it finally feels 'settled' to me. \n\nEven though part of me completely knows it’s already fine, I just keep doing it. Outside of my art, I haven't noticed anything like that at all.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the level of distress these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors cause you, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess I'm not entirely sure how to put a number on it. It's definitely stressful when it's happening in the studio, but... maybe a 3 or a 4? It's frustrating and it slows me down, but it's not like it's completely ruining my life or keeping me from functioning, you know? It's just a really annoying thing I have to deal with when I'm working.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're in conflict with your sense of self?\n\nPatient: I think... well, like I mentioned earlier, they definitely feel like they're in conflict with who I am. I don't want to be doing this, and I don't actually believe I'm dishonoring her. So, yeah, it doesn't feel like it's a part of my actual personality or my values at all. It just feels like this annoying habit or glitch that gets in the way when I'm trying to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unwanted, recurring thoughts outside of the thoughts about dishonoring your aunt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the stuff about my aunt. I haven't noticed any other thoughts like that popping up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something you'd expect to think, or do they feel like they're coming from a part of you that you don't identify with?\n\nPatient: Well, I think that's kind of what I meant just a minute ago. I definitely don't identify with them. It's not something I would ever expect myself to think, because I know I'm not actually trying to disrespect her. So... yeah, it just feels like it's coming from somewhere else. It really doesn't feel like me at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the urge to perform these checking behaviors when you're not thinking about your aunt or working on a memorial piece?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It really only happens when I'm working on those pieces. Like, I’ll be in the studio and I’ll end up rereading the label text, or... nudging the exact same objects on my work table. I'll check the layout photos over and over again until it finally feels 'settled' to me. \n\nEven though part of me completely knows it’s already fine, I just keep doing it. I don't really have the urge to do that stuff outside of work or when I'm not thinking about her, it's just... I end up losing a lot of studio time because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are interfering with your ability to enjoy or participate in activities outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, outside of work, not as much. It actually already cost me a grant deadline that I missed because I couldn't get the application materials together fast enough. And... I even had to back out of a gallery talk recently that I was really looking forward to doing, just because I was so behind on my pieces. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. When those thoughts happen, I get this... this really tense feeling. And if I try to just ignore it and walk away without checking the layout or the text, it's hard to focus on anything else. I just feel kind of on edge and uneasy until I finally go back and do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing these checking behaviors in other areas of your life, outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's pretty much just when I'm working on my art, especially pieces that feel really important or personal. It seems to be tied specifically to my studio work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are affecting your overall quality of life or relationships outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it just affects my work. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I was working on a memorial piece for my aunt recently, and I kept getting these awful, unwanted thoughts. I kept thinking I was going to dishonor her somehow. Like, if I placed a photo in the wrong spot or chose the wrong color, it would mean I didn't love her enough. And I just... I couldn't brush them off like normal creative doubts. They just kept coming back.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel the need to do anything to make these unwanted thoughts go away or to prevent them from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I'll end up rereading the label text, or just... nudging the same objects on my work table. And I check the layout photos over and over again. I basically have to keep doing it until things finally feel settled. It's frustrating because part of me knows it's already fine, and I'm really just losing studio time doing all this checking, but I still feel like I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you have these unwanted thoughts or when you're not able to perform your checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. When those thoughts happen, I get this... this really tense feeling. And if I try to just ignore it and walk away without checking the layout or the text, it's hard to focus on anything else. I just feel kind of on edge and uneasy until I finally go back and do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing these checking behaviors in other areas of your life, outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's pretty much just when I'm working on my art, especially pieces that feel really important or personal. I don't really have those kinds of thoughts or feel the need to check things over and over when I'm just going about my day-to-day life, like at home or out running errands. It seems to be tied specifically to my studio work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are affecting your overall quality of life or relationships outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it just affects my work. I mean, I'm still getting the majority of my art done, but... the extra time I lose in the studio because of all the checking is starting to have an impact. I actually missed a grant deadline recently because I was so caught up in making sure everything was perfect. And I ended up backing out of a gallery talk I really wanted to do because I just felt too behind and stressed about the work. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of opportunities, even if it's not ruining my personal relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something that come from your own beliefs and values, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean, they're in my head, so I know they are my own thoughts. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of my core beliefs or if they feel like they come from the outside. \n\nIt's just... this sudden worry that pops into my mind while I'm working. I wouldn't say it feels like someone else is putting the thoughts there, if that's what you mean. But beyond that, I guess I just haven't really noticed or thought about where exactly it feels like it's coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about possibly dishonoring your aunt feel like they align with your own beliefs and values, or do they feel alien or contradictory to who you are?\n\nPatient: I guess... I mean, I know they're my thoughts, like I said. But I wouldn't really say they line up with who I am or what I actually believe. It's more like... they just show up, and they feel really out of place. Like, I know I'm honoring her by making the art in the first place, so the thought that I'm somehow ruining it or disrespecting her doesn't make any logical sense to me. It's just a worry that gets stuck there, even though it goes against what I actually know is true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the need to perform any of these checking behaviors outside of your studio or when you're not working on your art?\n\nPatient: No, um... not really. It's pretty much just confined to the studio. Like, when I'm at home or just out doing regular things, I don't get that urge to go back and check things over and over. It really only happens when I'm working on those specific pieces. Outside of my art, I haven't noticed anything like that at all.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the level of distress these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors cause you, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess I'm not entirely sure how to put a number on it. I don't want to be doing this, and I don't actually believe I'm dishonoring her. So, yeah, it doesn't feel like it's a part of my actual personality or my values at all. It just feels like this annoying habit or glitch that gets in the way when I'm trying to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unwanted, recurring thoughts outside of the thoughts about dishonoring your aunt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the stuff about my aunt. I haven't noticed any other thoughts like that popping up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something you'd expect to think, or do they feel like they're coming from a part of you that you don't identify with?\n\nPatient: Well, I think that's kind of what I meant just a minute ago. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I was working on a memorial piece for my aunt recently, and I kept getting these awful, unwanted thoughts. I kept thinking I was going to dishonor her somehow. Like, if I placed a photo in the wrong spot or chose the wrong color, it would mean I didn't love her enough. And I just... I couldn't brush them off like normal creative doubts. They just kept coming back.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel the need to do anything to make these unwanted thoughts go away or to prevent them from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I'll end up rereading the label text, or just... nudging the same objects on my work table. And I check the layout photos over and over again. I basically have to keep doing it until things finally feel settled. It's frustrating because part of me knows it's already fine, and I'm really just losing studio time doing all this checking, but I still feel like I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you have these unwanted thoughts or when you're not able to perform your checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. When those thoughts happen, I get this... this really tense feeling. And if I try to just ignore it and walk away without checking the layout or the text, it's hard to focus on anything else. I just feel kind of on edge and uneasy until I finally go back and do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing these checking behaviors in other areas of your life, outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's pretty much just when I'm working on my art, especially pieces that feel really important or personal. I don't really have those kinds of thoughts or feel the need to check things over and over when I'm just going about my day-to-day life, like at home or out running errands. It seems to be tied specifically to my studio work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are affecting your overall quality of life or relationships outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it just affects my work. I mean, I'm still getting the majority of my art done, but... the extra time I lose in the studio because of all the checking is starting to have an impact. I actually missed a grant deadline recently because I was so caught up in making sure everything was perfect. And I ended up backing out of a gallery talk I really wanted to do because I just felt too behind and stressed about the work. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of opportunities, even if it's not ruining my personal relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something that come from your own beliefs and values, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean, they're in my head, so I know they are my own thoughts. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of my core beliefs or if they feel like they come from the outside. \n\nIt's just... this sudden worry that pops into my mind while I'm working. I wouldn't say it feels like someone else is putting the thoughts there, if that's what you mean. But beyond that, I guess I just haven't really noticed or thought about where exactly it feels like it's coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about possibly dishonoring your aunt feel like they align with your own beliefs and values, or do they feel alien or contradictory to who you are?\n\nPatient: I guess... I mean, I know they're my thoughts, like I said. But I wouldn't really say they line up with who I am or what I actually believe. It's more like... they just show up, and they feel really out of place. Like, I know I'm honoring her by making the art in the first place, so the thought that I'm somehow ruining it or disrespecting her doesn't make any logical sense to me. It's just a worry that gets stuck there, even though it goes against what I actually know is true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the need to perform any of these checking behaviors outside of your studio or when you're not working on your art?\n\nPatient: No, um... not really. It's pretty much just confined to the studio. Like, when I'm at home or just out doing regular things, I don't get that urge to go back and check things over and over. Outside of my art, I haven't noticed anything like that at all.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the level of distress these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors cause you, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess I'm not entirely sure how to put a number on it. It's just a really annoying thing I have to deal with when I'm working.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're in conflict with your sense of self?\n\nPatient: I think... well, like I mentioned earlier, they definitely feel like they're in conflict with who I am. It's just the stuff about my aunt. I haven't noticed any other thoughts like that popping up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something you'd expect to think, or do they feel like they're coming from a part of you that you don't identify with?\n\nPatient: Well, I think that's kind of what I meant just a minute ago. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I was working on a memorial piece for my aunt recently, and I kept getting these awful, unwanted thoughts. I kept thinking I was going to dishonor her somehow. Like, if I placed a photo in the wrong spot or chose the wrong color, it would mean I didn't love her enough. And I just... I couldn't brush them off like normal creative doubts. They just kept coming back.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel the need to do anything to make these unwanted thoughts go away or to prevent them from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I'll end up rereading the label text, or just... nudging the same objects on my work table. And I check the layout photos over and over again. I basically have to keep doing it until things finally feel settled. It's frustrating because part of me knows it's already fine, and I'm really just losing studio time doing all this checking, but I still feel like I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you have these unwanted thoughts or when you're not able to perform your checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. When those thoughts happen, I get this... this really tense feeling. And if I try to just ignore it and walk away without checking the layout or the text, it's hard to focus on anything else. I just feel kind of on edge and uneasy until I finally go back and do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing these checking behaviors in other areas of your life, outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's pretty much just when I'm working on my art, especially pieces that feel really important or personal. I don't really have those kinds of thoughts or feel the need to check things over and over when I'm just going about my day-to-day life, like at home or out running errands. It seems to be tied specifically to my studio work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are affecting your overall quality of life or relationships outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it just affects my work. I mean, I'm still getting the majority of my art done, but... the extra time I lose in the studio because of all the checking is starting to have an impact. I actually missed a grant deadline recently because I was so caught up in making sure everything was perfect. And I ended up backing out of a gallery talk I really wanted to do because I just felt too behind and stressed about the work. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of opportunities, even if it's not ruining my personal relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something that come from your own beliefs and values, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean, they're in my head, so I know they are my own thoughts. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of my core beliefs or if they feel like they come from the outside. \n\nIt's just... this sudden worry that pops into my mind while I'm working. I wouldn't say it feels like someone else is putting the thoughts there, if that's what you mean. But beyond that, I guess I just haven't really noticed or thought about where exactly it feels like it's coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about possibly dishonoring your aunt feel like they align with your own beliefs and values, or do they feel alien or contradictory to who you are?\n\nPatient: I guess... I mean, I know they're my thoughts, like I said. But I wouldn't really say they line up with who I am or what I actually believe. It's more like... they just show up, and they feel really out of place. Like, I know I'm honoring her by making the art in the first place, so the thought that I'm somehow ruining it or disrespecting her doesn't make any logical sense to me. It's just a worry that gets stuck there, even though it goes against what I actually know is true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the need to perform any of these checking behaviors outside of your studio or when you're not working on your art?\n\nPatient: No, um... not really. It's pretty much just confined to the studio. Like, when I'm at home or just out doing regular things, I don't get that urge to go back and check things over and over. It really only happens when I'm working on those specific pieces. Outside of my art, I haven't noticed anything like that at all.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the level of distress these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors cause you, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess I'm not entirely sure how to put a number on it. It's definitely stressful when it's happening in the studio, but... maybe a 3 or a 4? It's frustrating and it slows me down, but it's not like it's completely ruining my life or keeping me from functioning, you know? It's just a really annoying thing I have to deal with when I'm working.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're in conflict with your sense of self?\n\nPatient: I think... well, like I mentioned earlier, they definitely feel like they're in conflict with who I am. I don't want to be doing this, and I don't actually believe I'm dishonoring her. So, yeah, it doesn't feel like it's a part of my actual personality or my values at all. It just feels like this annoying habit or glitch that gets in the way when I'm trying to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unwanted, recurring thoughts outside of the thoughts about dishonoring your aunt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the stuff about my aunt. I haven't noticed any other thoughts like that popping up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something you'd expect to think, or do they feel like they're coming from a part of you that you don't identify with?\n\nPatient: Well, I think that's kind of what I meant just a minute ago. I definitely don't identify with them. It's not something I would ever expect myself to think, because I know I'm not actually trying to disrespect her. So... yeah, it just feels like it's coming from somewhere else. It really doesn't feel like me at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the urge to perform these checking behaviors when you're not thinking about your aunt or working on a memorial piece?\n\nPatient: No, not really. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.2, F41.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I was working on a memorial piece for my aunt recently, and I kept getting these awful, unwanted thoughts. I kept thinking I was going to dishonor her somehow. Like, if I placed a photo in the wrong spot or chose the wrong color, it would mean I didn't love her enough. And I just... I couldn't brush them off like normal creative doubts. They just kept coming back.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel the need to do anything to make these unwanted thoughts go away or to prevent them from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I'll end up rereading the label text, or just... nudging the same objects on my work table. And I check the layout photos over and over again. I basically have to keep doing it until things finally feel settled. It's frustrating because part of me knows it's already fine, and I'm really just losing studio time doing all this checking, but I still feel like I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you have these unwanted thoughts or when you're not able to perform your checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. When those thoughts happen, I get this... this really tense feeling. And if I try to just ignore it and walk away without checking the layout or the text, it's hard to focus on anything else. I just feel kind of on edge and uneasy until I finally go back and do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing these checking behaviors in other areas of your life, outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's pretty much just when I'm working on my art, especially pieces that feel really important or personal. I don't really have those kinds of thoughts or feel the need to check things over and over when I'm just going about my day-to-day life, like at home or out running errands. It seems to be tied specifically to my studio work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are affecting your overall quality of life or relationships outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, mostly it just affects my work. I mean, I'm still getting the majority of my art done, but... the extra time I lose in the studio because of all the checking is starting to have an impact. I actually missed a grant deadline recently because I was so caught up in making sure everything was perfect. And I ended up backing out of a gallery talk I really wanted to do because I just felt too behind and stressed about the work. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of opportunities, even if it's not ruining my personal relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something that come from your own beliefs and values, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean, they're in my head, so I know they are my own thoughts. But I haven't really thought about it in terms of my core beliefs or if they feel like they come from the outside. \n\nIt's just... this sudden worry that pops into my mind while I'm working. I wouldn't say it feels like someone else is putting the thoughts there, if that's what you mean. But beyond that, I guess I just haven't really noticed or thought about where exactly it feels like it's coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about possibly dishonoring your aunt feel like they align with your own beliefs and values, or do they feel alien or contradictory to who you are?\n\nPatient: I guess... I mean, I know they're my thoughts, like I said. But I wouldn't really say they line up with who I am or what I actually believe. It's more like... they just show up, and they feel really out of place. Like, I know I'm honoring her by making the art in the first place, so the thought that I'm somehow ruining it or disrespecting her doesn't make any logical sense to me. It's just a worry that gets stuck there, even though it goes against what I actually know is true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the need to perform any of these checking behaviors outside of your studio or when you're not working on your art?\n\nPatient: No, um... not really. It's pretty much just confined to the studio. Like, when I'm at home or just out doing regular things, I don't get that urge to go back and check things over and over. It really only happens when I'm working on those specific pieces. Outside of my art, I haven't noticed anything like that at all.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the level of distress these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors cause you, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess I'm not entirely sure how to put a number on it. It's definitely stressful when it's happening in the studio, but... maybe a 3 or a 4? It's frustrating and it slows me down, but it's not like it's completely ruining my life or keeping me from functioning, you know? It's just a really annoying thing I have to deal with when I'm working.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they're in conflict with your sense of self?\n\nPatient: I think... well, like I mentioned earlier, they definitely feel like they're in conflict with who I am. I don't want to be doing this, and I don't actually believe I'm dishonoring her. So, yeah, it doesn't feel like it's a part of my actual personality or my values at all. It just feels like this annoying habit or glitch that gets in the way when I'm trying to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unwanted, recurring thoughts outside of the thoughts about dishonoring your aunt?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the stuff about my aunt. I haven't noticed any other thoughts like that popping up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something you'd expect to think, or do they feel like they're coming from a part of you that you don't identify with?\n\nPatient: Well, I think that's kind of what I meant just a minute ago. I definitely don't identify with them. It's not something I would ever expect myself to think, because I know I'm not actually trying to disrespect her. So... yeah, it just feels like it's coming from somewhere else. It really doesn't feel like me at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the urge to perform these checking behaviors when you're not thinking about your aunt or working on a memorial piece?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It really only happens when I'm working on those pieces. Like, I’ll be in the studio and I’ll end up rereading the label text, or... nudging the exact same objects on my work table. I'll check the layout photos over and over again until it finally feels 'settled' to me. \n\nEven though part of me completely knows it’s already fine, I just keep doing it. I don't really have the urge to do that stuff outside of work or when I'm not thinking about her, it's just... I end up losing a lot of studio time because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are interfering with your ability to enjoy or participate in activities outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, outside of work, not as much. But it is definitely interfering with my career. I mean, I'm still getting the bulk of my work done, eventually, but... the extra time I lose doing all that checking in the studio has consequences. It actually already cost me a grant deadline that I missed because I couldn't get the application materials together fast enough. And... I even had to back out of a gallery talk recently that I was really looking forward to doing, just because I was so behind on my pieces. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the distress caused by these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors is mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I'd probably say mild. I mean, it's definitely frustrating, and like I said, it's getting in the way of my work... but it's not completely overwhelming my whole life. I'm not falling apart over it or anything. It's just really annoying. So, yeah, I guess mild makes the most sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are consistent with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what you mean by that. So... yeah, I wouldn't say it's part of who I am or what I believe in. It's just this frustrating thing I can't seem to stop doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something that you would normally think, or do they feel foreign to you?\n\nPatient: They definitely feel foreign. I mean, that's actually why I decided to come in. It really doesn't feel like me at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt the urge to perform these checking behaviors when you're not thinking about your aunt or working on a memorial piece?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It really only happens when I'm working on those pieces. Like, I’ll be in the studio and I’ll end up rereading the label text, or... nudging the exact same objects on my work table. I'll check the layout photos over and over again until it finally feels 'settled' to me. \n\nEven though part of me completely knows it’s already fine, I just keep doing it. I don't really have the urge to do that stuff outside of work or when I'm not thinking about her, it's just... I end up losing a lot of studio time because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are interfering with your ability to enjoy or participate in activities outside of your studio work?\n\nPatient: Well, outside of work, not as much. But it is definitely interfering with my career. I mean, I'm still getting the bulk of my work done, eventually, but... the extra time I lose doing all that checking in the studio has consequences. It actually already cost me a grant deadline that I missed because I couldn't get the application materials together fast enough. And... I even had to back out of a gallery talk recently that I was really looking forward to doing, just because I was so behind on my pieces. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the distress caused by these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors is mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I'd probably say mild. I mean, it's definitely frustrating, and like I said, it's getting in the way of my work... but it's not completely overwhelming my whole life. I'm not falling apart over it or anything. It's just really annoying. So, yeah, I guess mild makes the most sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors are consistent with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what you mean by that. I guess... no? It doesn't really have anything to do with my beliefs. \n\nI mean, I value my art and my time, and these habits are just getting in the way of both. So... yeah, I wouldn't say it's part of who I am or what I believe in. It's just this frustrating thing I can't seem to stop doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about dishonoring your aunt are something that you would normally think, or do they feel foreign to you?\n\nPatient: They definitely feel foreign. I mean, that's actually why I decided to come in. When I was working on my aunt's memorial piece, I just kept getting these awful, unwanted thoughts that I was going to dishonor her somehow. \n\nIt was like... if I made one wrong photo placement or chose the wrong color, it would mean I didn't love her enough. Normally, I have creative doubts with my art, but I can just brush them off. But I couldn't brush these off like normal doubts at all. So, no, they really don't feel like my normal thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you might harm someone or something, or that something terrible might happen, when you're not able to perform your checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I'm going to hurt anyone, or like someone's going to die if I don't check my work. It's not like that. It's just... more of an intense anxiety that the piece itself is ruined, or that I'm being disrespectful. But I don't have fears about causing physical harm or anything terrible happening to people.\n\nDoctor: Are these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It hasn't caused any issues with my friends or family. It's mostly just something I deal with on my own when I'm working in my studio. I mean, maybe I'm a little more stressed out lately because of it, but it's not really spilling over into my relationships or keeping me from seeing people.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to meet your deadlines or deliver your work on time due to these unwanted thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: For the most part, I'm still getting most of my work done. But... the extra time I lose in the studio is starting to have an impact. It actually cost me a grant deadline recently because I just couldn't get the application together in time. And, um, I even had to back out of a gallery talk that I had really wanted to do. So, yeah, it is definitely getting in the way of some of my professional commitments."
